authorities, 4) Scientific community, and 5) General Public.
Impact 4: More effective market uptake of RE to support commercialization & provide input to policy making.
SuRE aims to reduce the observed hesitance to enter and/or invest in FPV and to increase societal acceptance of
FPV. The consortium has identified uncertainty as a hindrance for greater market uptake of FPV. There is
uncertainty related to sustainability, lifetime, and yield for FPV power plants. SuRE address all these three aspects
in the generalized WPs with the aim of making more knowledge publicly available for industry stakeholders.
SuRE, through substantial effort in WP7, also has a strong commitment to communicate with European citizens
and policy makers. The results related to sustainability are considered to be of special relevance for a broad
audience.
The results are linked to HLO4 in this proposal and will be accomplished through the work in WP1, 2, 3, and 7.
KPIs: 1) Facilitate communication between public and FPV providers through artistic platforms 2) Streamline
LCOE calculations to accelerate investment process. 3) Paper detailing inputs of EYA for FPV in PVsyst. 4)
Publication of sensitivity of FPV plant decisions on LCOE. Target groups: 1) Main end-users, 2) FPV industry,
3) Public authorities, 4) Scientific community, and 5) GP.
Impact 5: Increase knowledge on the environmental impacts of RE techs along their lifetime and value chains.
Outcome 3: Minimize the environmental impact of FPV technology for inland and offshore waters.
A result from SuRE will be a set of guidelines including relevant parameters, methods for monitoring, and
reference values, to assess the direct impact of FPV on aquatic systems and biodiversity. The impact of FPV on
ecosystem biodiversity will be quantified, and optimizations and mitigation measures will be investigated to
further reduce negative impact and enhance positive impact on the environment. These results will enable policy
makers and concession granting bodies a solid scientific foundation for regulating FPV deployment, do proper and
efficient permitting, and expand application of FPV while minimizing any negative environmental impact and
maximizing the potential positive impacts on local environment and biodiversity.
These results from SuRE are linked primarily with HLO3 and will be accomplished through the work in WP1, 4,
5, and 6. KPIs: 1) Protocol for measuring hydrological and ambient variables to model the interaction of FPV
with waterbodies, 2) Published results on biodiversity mitigation measures, and on effect of coverage ratios for
European waterbody types, 3) Internally agreed pre-normative procedures for assessing FPV sustainability form
basis of published guidelines for sustainability monitoring of FPV power plants. Target groups: 1) Main end-
users, 2) FPV industry, 3) Public authorities, 4) Scientific community, and 5) GP.

2.1.2 The Key Impact Pathways

 Scientific Impact             Creating high-quality knowledge

In SURE the four leading research institutes on FPV in Europe is collaborating with the leading FPV technology
providers to push the forefront of FPV science and technology. FPV is a relatively new technology, and access to
high quality scientific literature based on field data and monitoring is very scarce. There is a great need for more
accessible, openly available and high-quality scientific, and popular scientific, literature. In SuRE the research
partners will analyse production and monitoring data from a number of commercial FPV sites. We expect that
much of this information can and will be shared in peer reviewed publications and will have a huge impact on the
knowledge base for FPV. The publishing work will predominantly be carried out in WP1, 2 and 3. List of papers
with preliminary titles is provided in the Dissemination Plan in Section 2.2.
Indicator:Number of peer-reviewed papers & conference proc. 9 peer-reviewed + 4 conference proceedings

                                            Strengthening human capital in R&I

With the tremendous upscaling of RE deployment, there is a lack of skilled personell in the business. Technical
careers and education in solar jobs must be promoted as a matter of priority to solve the sector's issue of lacking
skilled labor. In SuRE, one PhD student (Deltares/Utrecht University) is fully funded by the project, 4 PhD
students are associated with the project and 12 Master's students will be educated (University of Freiburg,
University of Oslo and Utrecth University). Fraunhofer regularly offers workshops and training for the FPV
industry. This will be an excellent platform to disseminate the results from FPV to ensure uptake of updated
knowledge. Through the project, the topic of FPV will be introduced at the University of Oslo (UiO) through the
courses TEK5330 and TEK9330. The course is taught by personell from IFE and is currently covering general
aspects of PV systems, including BIPV, but not yet FPV. A 3-hour lecture on FPV will be developed and included
in the course. The course reaches 15-20 students per year. The lecture material will be developed; the lectures
will continue after the project's end. Fraunhofer will introduce FPV to the Solar Energy module in the Sustainable

                            Fostering diffusion of knowledge and Open Science

                   Strengthening the uptake of research and innovation in society

Our approach to strengthen the market uptake is to reduce uncertainties and thereby reduce hesitancy of investing

in FPV power plants, and to contribute to disseminate knowledge to a broad audience to reduce conflict, and

thereby accelerate FPV. Reduced uncertainty includes improved accuracy of EYA and streamlined LCOE

calculations. To reduce conflict, we seek to foster multilateral dialogue among residents, permitting bodies,

research teams, FPV industry. WP7 Communication and Dissemination integrates the FPV piloting processes

with public engagement through art. The WP leader, Compáz, combines their vision for both art and PV to

maximize uptake of new technology by ensuring FPV suits both producers and consumers.

 Technolo cal/econom c Impact  Generating innovation-based growth

The SuRE consortium provides a uniqe arena for innovation-based growth in the European FPV industry. Projects

where firms are attempting to forge new ties in new collaborative R&D efforts and make R&D strategies that are

more accessible to other researchers and firms, are more likely to successfully diffuse and exploit the knowledge

generated by the project. Projects with such attributes are more likely to yield economic benefits, all other things

being equal, that extend beyond the firm directly engaged in the project. In SuRE the leading companies within

FPV in Europe are working together to enforce the world leading competence and position of the European FPV

industry and increase the deployment rate of European FPV technology, in Europe and globally. Through SuRE

a number of innovative products, processes and methods will be developed, exploited by the partners in the

consortium and efficienty communicated to the wide FPV industry, governance and public. The list of new

producs, processes and methods developed in SuRE is provided in Table 1.1.3.

methods from FP & Intellectual Property Rights applications methods. See Table 1.1.3 for details.

                                       Creating more and better jobs

New solar power generation capacities are highly cost-competitive, but yet the most job-intensive among low

carbon and RE technologies. PV creates about 6 jobs per MW of capacity in manufacturing, construction, and
installation and 1.2 - 4.8 in operating and maintenance23, which means 7 to 11 FTEs/MW deployed. As a

significant fraction will be employed within production of cells and modules, we will assume that only 10% of

the FTEs/MW is employed within the part of the value chain covered by BayWa/ZIM and CTI/LKY, i.e., 1

FTE/MW. Sunlit covers a smaller and less labor-intensive part of the value chain than CTI/LKY and ZIM/BayWa

as they do not deploy the systems. Market predictions for FPV vary greatly, also indicating the sensitivity to cost

predictions for FPV, but an increase of 25% annually is in the middle of the span of the predictions, and the

needed annual growth rate of PV if we are to meet zero emission in 2050. However, these predictions are highly

uncertain. The combined results of LCOE reductions, increased application offshore, and reduced uncertainty

related to permitting and sustainability-concerns, all outcomes of SuRE, will lead to a more rapid market growth

than the benchmark scenario of 25%. ZIM/BayWa's SuRE­target is to reduce BoS cost with 15%, translating

into a ~ 5% reduction in LCOE. Combining the LCOE reductions with the plans for enhanced sustainability,

dissemination and exploitation, CTI estimates that their developments in SuRE will increase their market share

to 20% by 2028. Sunlit plans to have an establish production-line of 11 MW by mid-2024, and an upscaling to

455 MW by the end of 2026. FTEs in production of other components and related value chains not included in

these estimates. The estimates only look at additional MW and FTEs beyond the benchmark 25% market growth.

2.1.4 Scale and significance of the project's contribution to outcomes and impacts

The International Energy Agency (IEA) reported that for the world to reach the goal of net zero emissions by 2050

there will have to be an annual average solar energy generation growth of 25%1. In 2020, solar generation increased

22%, resulting in the IEA categorizing solar photovoltaics (PV) as 'more effort needed' 24. FPV is a clear addition to

the PV industry, adding to overall growth while avoiding increasing competition with land use. To do FPV correctly,

however, requires the broad view of SuRE. The SuRE Consortium is capable of making a remarkable impact on

the LCOE of FPV power plants. Frequently hailed as the third pillar of the global PV market, FPV are gaining

traction around the world The total installed capacity has surpassed 3 GW and the annual installed capacity of FPV

is currently around 1 GW. The cost of FPV varies substantially, depending on market and local conditions, but

assuming a cost of 1.5 /W, the current value of the annual FPV

market is around 1.5 billion. However, the variation in the           Realizing the 5-10% LCOE reductions
estimates of growth and future market shares for FPV is striking.     targeted in SuRE will make FPV more cost
Published estimates of the annual CAGR varies from 5% to              competitive and more likely to reach the high
43%25. If we assume a value of 1.5 billion in 2021 and a 25%          market growth scenarios. By 2030, the
linear growth rate, the global, annual FPV market will reach 6 GW     difference in deployment resulting from an
of annual installed capacity and 9 billion in 2030. Significantly     annual growth rate of 20% versus an annual
higher estimates also exist, such as from a USA government report     growth rate of 30% will be more than 10 GW.
of $27.7 billion by the end of 203126. The great variation in

predicted market size and growth rates also indicate the sensitivity

of the growth to cost predictions for FPV. Analysis shows that FPV currently is 12-20% more expensive than

GPV27,28, and the rate of cost reductions will significantly influence the market size.

                                                            Part B - Page 24 of 45
Call: [HORIZON-CL5-2023-D3-01-03] -- [Floating PV Systems]

                                                                 EU Grants: Application form (HE RIA and IA): V3.2 ­ 15.11.2022
                                                            Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)4725898 - 01/07/2024

The uncertainty of the predictions highlights the sensitivity of FPV deployment with respect to future costs and
policies. Sensitivity analysis shows that installed FPV system cost is most sensitive to float costs and module
efficiency, followed by local loads28. Depending on system size, the structural balance of system (SBoS) cost
constitutes 25% ­30% of the total CAPEX. SuRE targets all the most critical parameters to reduce LCOE. The
main technology developments in SuRE are directed towards reducing SBoS costs. In addition, we address
optimization of module cost versus efficiency for FPV applications, and more accurate predictions of loads to reduce
overengineering. For the business analysis carried out for this proposal, the consortium has predicted an annual
growth rate of 25% as a benchmark scenario. The partners have analyzed how they expect the developments in SuRE
to impact their growth beyond that of only following the market, as shown in Section 2.1.2. under "creating more
and better jobs". We have not attempted to evaluate how SuRE can impact the growth of the total market for SuRE,
although given the capacities, cost reduction and sustainability goals of the partners, it is highly likely that SuRE will
not only increase the market shares of the partners, but also increase the deployment rate of FPV and expand the
global market. Through competitive advantages related to LCOE, sustainability and synergies in exploitation, which
will be realized through SuRE, all partners target a growth that is significantly greater than the benchmark market
growth of 25%.

SuRE will enable the further deployment of FPV in Europe will be implemented with a substantially higher level
of circularity and a substantial lower risk for the environment. Despite the potential, European countries have been
slow to embrace FPV. The dominating market has been Asia. This is partly due to a lack of empirical data, but mostly
due to the specific conditions that must be met ­ in terms of design, materials, system components, operation, and
environmental impact ­ when working with bodies of water29. Long-term environmental sustainability is a vital
requirement that FPV plants must fulfill if they are going to be approved for use in many European markets. The lack
of knowledge on sustainability also infers that economic analysis lacks the full value proposition of FPV systems in
detail because lifetime analysis of cost and revenues encompassing the impact of the energy-water-nexus is required
to understand the complete economic feasibility of FPV applications28. SuRE is ambitious with respect to providing
accurate and validated models for the impact of FPV on biodiversity and the local environment. The knowledge will
be made available through accessible guidelines, directly helping the public authorities and regulatory bodies to make
decisions based on high-quality data. Decreased costs, reduced uncertainties, and improved sustainability of the FPV
systems, as achieved through SuRE are the factors needed for more rapid deployment in Europe. The potential is
immense. In addition, the production capacity for both MetSolar, CTI, ZIM and Sunlit will be greatly enhanced, and
CTI, MetSolar and Sunlit all plan new production lines to exploit the results in SuRE. The current module production
capacity in Europe is limited, and additional production capacity is another important factor to enhance the security
and autonomy of energy supply in Euorpe.

2.2.2 Exploitation Strategy and measures

SuRE will make a strong effort and thus has dedicated activities to develop a business framework and market analysis
leading to the exploitation and further implementation. All partners will be directly involved in these activities

                                                            Part B - Page 26 of 45
Call: [HORIZON-CL5-2023-D3-01-03] -- [Floating PV Systems]

                                                                 EU Grants: Application form (HE RIA and IA): V3.2 ­ 15.11.2022
                                                            Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)4725898 - 01/07/2024

bringing relevant options for market uptake. The role each partner plays in this strategy is different according to its
nature. Exploitation activities will be carried out primarily in the individual companies, supervised by the project
coordinator. WP7 will create effective measures for preparing the market uptake of the results, while the individual
WPs deals with the development of the solutions themselves. The solutions will reach TRL6-7, thus, European
regulations and directives will be fulfilled for the pilots, shortening market uptake. The SuRE consortium has defined
an overall exploitation strategy describing how we will work to ensure that usable knowledge to be generated during
the project, based on the three main steps below.

 Results Identification: Identification and definition of the key exploitable results (KER) of the project. This step
    also includes the identification of the level of interest in the different results and the identification of the added
    value of the project to boost further scientific and exploitation developments.

 Individual Partners' Exploitation Strategy: Definition of the partners' exploitation plans, including the market
    potential, the added value, the target end-users, the potential competitors, the expected impact on the partners'
    portfolio, a tentative timetable and the opportunities and barriers for the exploitation action.

 Consortium level Exploitation Strategy: Definition of common exploitation plan, based on a combination of the
    Individual Partners' Plans according to clustering criteria, including similar or common exploitation actions
    declared by the partners in their individual plans; the application domains considered for the exploitation; a
    consideration on the benefit expected from each exploitation actions.

Carrying out the exploitation strategy will lead to a concrete a periodically updated exploitation plan. This will
facilitate the identification of new business opportunities and associated business models throughout the project. To
identify KERs and select which results to pursue, feasibility studies to analyze the technical, financial, commercial
and organizational feasibility of the proposed product will be useful. In SuRE, the main innovations and identified
KERs are related to development of FPV technology. In the following, a preliminary feasibility study and business
plan focused on FPV floater technology is provided.

2.2.2.1 Feasibility Study

Technical Feasibility. In Table 1.1.2, Section 1.1.2, 1.13 and 1.2 the technical feasibility of the planned innovations,
the challenges and methods used to overcome them are thoroughly described.

Financial Feasibility. For the purpose and duration of SuRE, all partners have the capital needed to fulfil their
obligations and leverage as tabulated in the budget. Sunlit will need investors to realize the planned ramp up of the
production line in Norway. For this purpose, the Innovation Fund is a very relevant sources for funding. The next
deadline for small investments in the Innovation Fund is fall 2023 fits well with Sunlit's first upscaling. For the
technology developments planned in SuRE for CTI and Sunlit SuRE, funding for up-scaling will be required at a
later stage (~2025-2026). Sunlit has significant experience with capital-raising and already has a network of
subcontractors with capacity to deliver equipment for up-scaling. For the duration of the SuRE project the CTI and
LKY have the necessary capital to realize the plans, including technology developments, the first production and
pilots. For up-scaling, the production of the new technology, it will be necessary with capital raising. The Innovation
Fund is one of several potential sources for this funding. It may also be that subcontracting and renting will be the
best option, depending on available interest rates and conditions. BayWa, ZIM, SABIC, Metsolar and Compaz, have
the necessary capital and infrastructure to realize the plans in SURE, including technology developments and pilot.
For larger scale implementation and production of modules designed for FPV applications, MetSolar may require
capital to invest in new tools and/or upscaling. Needs for further investments will depend on the results from SuRE.

Market Feasibility. The market potential for cost-competitive, and sustainable FPV technologies is immense and
growing. We need to keep increasing the deployment rate of PV for many years to come if we are to meet average
EU policy goals1. And as PV implementation expands FPV provides a vast potential of increased deployment without
conflicting with other uses of land. Published estimates of the annual CAGR varies from 5% to 43%25. FPV is also
predicted to be the segment of PV Market to grow at the highest CAGR from 2020 to 2025. A more thorough analysis
of the market and market shares of the partners is provided in Section 2.1.4.

Organizational Feasibility. The partners and value chains are extremely well positioned to manage the extensive,
foreseen growth. The technical knowledge, business insight, and value chains of CTI and ZIM already are world
leading. Concrete plans for upscaling will be revised in the beginning of the project period. Sunlit has is currently
working to scale up a very promising, patented technology and do their first scale up and investments in new
production line with capacity 11 MW/year by early 2024. The founders of the company have many years of
experience from the FPV business and from other start-ups prior to founding Sunlit. They will also benefit
significantly from the deep knowledge of the FPV industry in the SuRE consortium.

2.2.2.2 Preliminary Business Plan
Business models are the main instrument to support the exploitation of SuRE's results. A business model creates
value for companies by identifying how to convert their products into profit. At the proposal level the Consortium
has decided to make use of the CANVAS model as a key strategic management tool allowing the description of a
preliminary Business Plan for the three FPV technology providers and value chains. This will be further used,
individualized, and improved as SuRE progresses. The Business Model Canvas (BMC) explains a business model
graphically, by dividing it into 9 basic modules that reflect the logic that a company follows to raise revenue, covering
the 4 main business areas: customers, value proposition offered, infrastructure and economic viability ­ finance. A
first CANVAS model analysis has been performed for SuRE and is shown in Fig. 9.

2.2.2.3 Exploitation Plan

Detailed individual exploitation plans for all partners will be developed within the first six months of the project
period. A preliminary individual exploitation plan with the identified KERs is provided in the table below.

 CTI KERs: New FPV technology and new product for efficient FPV maintenance.

 currently at TRL 3 and will be developed to TRL 6 by the end of the project (5 MW pilot will be TRL 5).
 Development will be done with the first off-taker, LKY, the four research institutes and a few central partners
 and subcontractors, one of which is SABIC. Quantified measurements from the 5 MW pilot will be important for

 makers, General public. Main sales channels: CTI sales department.

 BayWa KERs: Improved business case and extended range of applications for FPV projects.

 will make FPV more competitive and more appealing. This will be actively used in conversations with waterbody

owners, drinking-water utilities, hydropower operators and public authorities to increase their awareness and to

Sunlit Sea KER: Improved integration of floater and module for FPV application
Sunlit has developed an FPV based on an aluminum float with a solar panel laminated on top, serially connected
in a string of floats. In SuRE we improve the product by reducing aluminum usage, strengthening the float to be
more durable and open for use of recycled aluminum, assessing how the float handles rough sea and wind,
improving the heat transfer to the water, and piloting proposed changes with sensors in a wave tank. The project
will enable reduction in LCOE of up to 60% and scaling production from ~11 MW/year in 2024 to ~ 455 MW/year
by the project end. It will enable certification of the design criteria for the product and thus open new markets for
Sunlit. Target groups: 1) Main end users; Utility companies/PV developers, Investors 3) Public authorities 5)
General public. Main sales channels: direct dialogue with main investors and potential customers.
Metsolar KER: Design of PV module tailored for FPV.
Metsolar is a custom PV module manufacturer and sees FPV as an application with great potential. The work in
SuRE will be essential to quantify this potential by evaluating cost-efficiency of potential mitigation measures for
FPV-specific stressors. MetSolar currently manufactures modules for Sunlit. Sunlit's scale-up plans would make
them one of MetSolar's largest customers by project end. Cost optimizations and mitigation measures for FPV
modules in general, and Sunlit's FPV technology, will be focus areas in MetSolar's exploitation plan. Changes to
MetSolar's production line to allow more cost-efficient modules with larger cell-size is a likely optimization step
to reduce cost of Sunlit's FPV technology that can also be exploited in MetSolar's other markets. Target groups:
1) Main end-users; Sunlit, PV industry. Main sales channels: direct dialogue with main investors and customers.
Sabic KER: New low CO2 processes and products for plastic FPV components.
Sabic sees that the FPV market has strong growth potential. Sabic wants to achieve via SuRE a strong position to
offer materials solutions for more sustainable FPV (improved circularity, lower CFP). The exploitation strategy
of Sabic consists of (1) direct marketing towards FPV system providers, (2) influencing permit criteria by
disseminating options on improved circularity towards permitting authorities. Target groups: 2) FPV industry.
Main Sales channels: direct dialogue and development with potential customers.
IFE, TNO, Fraunhofer KERs: Predictive energy yield models, accelerated stress tests, sustainability guidelines
SuRE establishes the cooperation of the three leading European institutes in the field of FPV. For IFE, TNO and
Fraunhofer, SuRE strengthens the partnership with the leading FPV companies in Europe and confirms the strong
collaboration with SMEs in the field. IFE, TNO and Fraunhofer will gain valuable scientific and technical know-
how that will allow the institutes to get an even stronger profile in the FPV field. The research institutes will exploit
the project results through: i) Being the R&D partner for FPV industries; ii) Being the knowledge hub for public
bodies; iii) Dissemination of results, create follow-up projects for next step innovations, also towards offshore
FPV.
Deltares KERs: Sustainability Guidelines, Software
SuRE strengthen Deltares already leading position as knowledge hub and partner within sustainable deployment
of FPV. Deltares also have a clear exploitation strategy for their software development, with the `dare to share'
principle. Deltares distribute Delft3D as 1) open-source code and 2) as a compiled version via a service package.

Joint exploitation plan
The joint exploitation plan will be completed, as a part of the overall exploitation plan, within the first 6 months of
SuRE. SuRE is an ambitious and solutions-oriented project that can bring to market more affordable and sustainable
FPV technologies. The SuRE consortium will make use of the Horizon Results Platform, a system developed by the
European Commission to help promote the exploitation of the results from the R&I framework programmes. Using
this platform will allow the partners to publish and promote uptake of their results towards the key target groups of
SuRE. The key outcomes of will also enter the Horizon Results Booster (HRB), an initiative of the European
Commission whose aim is bringing a continual stream of innovation to the market whilst maximizing the impact of
public funded research within the EU. Through the HRB, SuRE will secure a larger Impact through an extended
Dissemination and Exploitation, heading the research activities towards strong societal impact whilst concretizing
the value of R&I activity for societal challenges.

2.2.2.4 IPR Strategy
Because SuRE comprises separate value chains of ostensible competitors, but whose common aim is to grow
European FPV activities, it is critical to identify and isolate innovations which need to remain within one entity.
Therefore, ownership and IPR management will allow each partner to innovate while protecting their IPR as set in
the Consortium Agreement. Nonetheless, partners have already achieved a preliminary agreement on the IP strategy
where each partner owns its IP background. The consortium has identified three levels of IP which will be created
from the project: (i) Individual and joint IP, which belongs to individual partners or is jointly owned by partners
working in a particular task and is restricted to those partners; (ii) Generic IP, which can be used by all partners of
the consortium; (iii) Publicly available IP which will be published on the SuRE website and made available with no
restrictions. Provisions for use of IP background will be determined during the commercialization strategy. Insights
that enhance partners' services or enable the launch of new solutions within partners' strategic go-to-market plan will
be kept private and protected via non-registered Soft IP measures (confidential information etc.).

2.2.3 Communication Strategy and Measures
The communication strategy of SuRE involves specific measures to promote the project itself and the results attained,
with the mission to reach a broad audience. We will promote outreach and networking activities by collaborating
with other relevant networks and projects to create joint dissemination activities thus further increasing the project's
outreach. The communication and dissemination analysis also includes engaging and collaborating with our listed
Target Groups as detailed in Section 2.1.1, as input to the concrete measures in the CDP. Common information and
dissemination, sharing upon invitation by CINEA, to used increase the visibility and synergies between Horizon
Europe supported actions. The CDP will be fine-tuned and closely monitored by the advisory board on a yearly basis.

                                                            The SuRE work structure promotes innovation

                                                            development for all FPV segments while

                                                            promoting targeted development of three

                                                            commercial technologies. A members-only

                                                            section of the SuRE website serves as a common

                                                            platform for formal and informal

                                                            communication. Formal project resources, such

                                                            as contracts, planning, meeting minutes, and

                                                            templates will be posted there. Project workflow

                                                            and quality control will be shared internally as

                                                            openly as possible, as closed as necessary, to

                                                            foster transparent project management. Leaders

                                                            of WPs 1-3 & 7-8 have recent experience of

                                                            similar leadership responsibilities in EU

projects. Leaders of WPs 4-6 lead internal and public R&D projects, and will receive guidance from the coordinator,

internal and external assistants, and via the collaboration baked in to SuRE. All WPs run the full duration of SuRE.

Work distribution among WPs allows for decentralized activities and responsibilities while holding clear reporting
and monitoring channels to align project management with achieving SuRE targets. Its structure is organized to reach
the objectives by fostering FPV growth overall and applying that mindset to 3 commercial technologies. Figure 10
shows the SuRE management structure. Three bodies will promote smooth management while providing
communication spaces for partners to express and discuss concerns and exchange project information: General
Assembly (GA), Steering Committee (SC), and Innovation at Commercialization Board (ICB). In addition, 3 external
experts, from Dutch national water authority, DNV, and Total Energies, respectively, have agreed to serve as an
Advisory Board (AB) to SuRE. The advisory board will be an organ to discuss fine-tuning of and uptake of results
in the broader FPV industry, for review of material, and a source of knowledge and connections with other ongoing
initiatives within FPV.
General Assembly (GA). The GA facilitates WP coordination and opportunities to discuss major changes to the
working plan. One representative from each partner will sit on the GA, meeting 4 times a year. It is the decision-
making body for major SuRE issues like changes of coordinator, work plan, budget, partners, IPR considerations,
and other general non-technical matters.
Steering Committee (SC). The SC is the operational management body and is responsible for efficient
implementation. The SuRE coordinator, WP leaders, and the commercial members of the ICB constitute the SC. The
SC works under the GA and assists, makes proposals for, and reports to, the GA. It monitors and ensures technical
quality progress, approves progress reports, coordinates review of deliverables, assesses milestones, and moderates
technical issues that span multiple WPs. The SC monitors ethics in SuRE.
Innovation and Commercialization Board (ICB). The ICB supports knowledge transfer to end-users, elevating
exploitation and commercialization opportunities that allow the IPR owners to fulfil SuRE Outcomes. Knowledge
transfer plans will be developed, according to type, application, IP conditions, and end users. ICB participation is

                                                                          mandatory for commercial partners, and optional for
                                                                          others; maximum 1 participant per partner. The
                                                                          coordinator is the ICB chairwoman.

                                                                          3.1 Work plan and resources
                                                                          As shown in Figure 9, WPs 1-3 push FPV boundaries
                                                                          in Sustainability (compassionate FPV), Reliability

                                                                          The work here is generalizable across FPV, pushing
                                                                          boundaries for the whole field, yet also includes
                                                                          participation of the commercial partners. In the
                                                                          interior, WPs 4-6 apply the generalizable methods to
                                                                          the 3 specific FPV technologies of CTI, ZIM and
                                                                          Sunlit. The work here is specific, yet also includes
                                                                          participation of the research institutes. Maximal
                                                                          information exchange along the circumference of WPs
                                                                          1-3 and radially into WPs 4-6 is how we can reduce
CAPEX, OPEX, and LCOE while increasing environmental benefit. Minimal information exchange among the

SuRE has 12 partners from 6 European countries, composed of 4 research institutes and 8 companies, as presented
in Chapter 1. The project will build two 5 MW FPV demonstrators plus one smaller, yet commercially relevant,
demonstrator. The pilot sites will be situated in Belgium (CTI/LKY, building and connection permits in place),
Netherlands (BayWa) and Norway (Sunlit). As we describe in the introduction Sec. 1.1.1, every member of SuRE
works actively in FPV. The research partners in SuRE are at the international forefront of FPV research and already
collaborate actively with the FPV industry, including SuRE partners. This is also evident from the list of FPV related
projects in Part A of this proposal. The institutes have recognized publications within the field of FPV and are
regularly invited to present their work at international conferences, workshops, and fairs. CTI is the second largest
producer of FPV technology globally measured by installed capacity, ZIM/BayWa is the leading FPV technology
provider/installer in Europe with a market share of 60%, and Sunlit have patented a promising, novel, FPV technology
which they are rapidly scaling up. SABIC and Metsolar support FPV technology development by providing necessary
expertise in the components. All partners are commercially active, R&D active, and committed to environmental
positivity of FPV. Compáz, with experience at the nexus of communication-public engagement-solar energy, puts
SuRE in the limelight to showcase the technical, social, and environmental focus of our work. We are prepared, and
exceptionally well positioned, to solve the challenges posed in the call: elevate the environmental position of FPV,
expand the application and decrease the LCOE. Every partner in SuRE has worked with at least one other partner on
previous FPV activities, as shown in Part A. SuRE has been built up based on competence, dialogue and close
cooperation between the partners, without mediators or consultants taking care of writing the proposal. The project
starts from a good understanding of the partners' roles, tasks, and responsibilities and all of them have actively
contributed to shaping the activities of SuRE.

IFE has experience as coordinator in H2020 and Horizon Europe, and has a dedicated European Affairs Department,
who will assist on financial matters and reporting. IFE's Coordination Team allocated for this project will consist of
3 people, including principal scientist, Dr. Josefine Selj as technical coordinator, 1 European advisor (who will
manage the financial and EU reporting aspects) and the Head of European Affairs and Business Development of IFE.
This setup allows effective contact with the European Commission while enabling scientific progress. Organization
of SuRE WPs, as described in Sec 3.1, is designed to allow all partners to complement one another. Major laboratory
and production infrastructure are in place at the sites required; SuRE is a method to share expertise and resources
without needing to double-up on equipment or training. Such collaborative exchanges are baked into the project
design as given in the WP description in Table 3.1b.

Description of the affiliated companies.

Laketricity: Ciel et Terre International and Laketricity are two sister companies with different activities. Laketricity
is a development company with the aim of get access to land, clear out all authorization process, secure the access to
the grid or a relevant point of consumption, sign the best power supply agreement and reach financial close. Ciel et
Terre international is a technology provider with the aim of developing floating solar technologies, producing, and
selling it, giving construction, anchoring, and maintenance services.

During the project, Laketricity will thus perform tasks related to project development (for the pilot and also more
generally tasks related to environmental impact, project social acceptance, electrical production forecast and
monitoring, LCOE) whereas CTI will perform tasks related to technology development (design, simulations, tests,
performances, pilot parts production, pilot assembly and testing, LCA).

GROENLEVEN BV: Groenleven BV is a sister company of BayWa R.E. Solar Projects GMBH located in the
Netherlands. Groenleven BV is building Floating-PV projects and has a track record of over 200 MWp of realized
projects. The company Groenleven BV will support in the sure PV project with their experience, knowledge and
open questions which can be addressed in the Sure PV project. Topics in the project which we can profit from the
support respectively work are topics like work on elaborate on environmental aspects, anchoring and mooring design
and feasibility in execution. Especially for difficult water bodies like near shore or bracket water we will get big
learning from the rougher conditions from the Netherland. Also experience of realized projects will bring benefits
into investigation into CAPEX reduction.

BAYWA RE PROGETTI SRL: BAYWA RE PROGETTI SRL is a sister company of BayWa R.E. Solar Projects
GMBH located in Italy. The Product management of FPV is partly located in Italy (Michele Tagliapietra Head of
Product Management Floating-PV). Within in the Sure PV project BAYWA RE PROGETTI will work on Project
management, internal work organization, and dissemination and outreach actvities.
